Clashes continued west and south of Avdiivka in March and April 2024, particularly along the [Tonenke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonenke)-[Orlivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlivka,_Ocheretyne_settlement_hromada,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast)-[Berdychi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berdychi) line[^cite_note-305] and the [Vodiane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodiane,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast)-[Pervomaiske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pervomaiske,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast) front. By 1 March, the Russians had a foothold on the eastern outskirts of [Berdychi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berdychi), had entered central [Orlivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orlivka,_Ocheretyne_settlement_hromada,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), and were threatening the northern and eastern flanks of [Tonenke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonenke).[^cite_note-AJ6324-302] The Russians later claimed to have captured Orlivka on 19 March and Tonenke on 21 March.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307] On 12 March, the Russian defence ministry claimed the capture of [Nevelske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nevelske), a village located on the southern flank of Pervomaiske and less than 15 kilometres (9.3 miles) north of [Krasnohorivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnohorivka).[^cite_note-308] The Russians claimed to have captured Vodiane on 5 April and reportedly captured Pervomaiske by 10 April.[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310]

On 14–16 April, Russian forces began expanding Avdiivka's northern flank, capturing the Zarya [dachas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dacha) and swiftly moving on to establish a foothold in [Ocheretyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ocheretyne), creating a salient. Russian troops [exploited the breach in defences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Ocheretyne), capturing Ocheretyne by 28 April.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312][^cite_note-euro2442024-313][^cite_note-314] Russian forces expanded the salient by quickly capturing the villages of [Novobakhmutivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novobakhmutivka,_Solovyiv_village_council) by 28 April and [Soloviove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soloviove,_Donetsk_Oblast) by 1 May along Ocheretyne's southern flank.[^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316] The Russians were concurrently advancing on a second axis northwards, east of the Ocheretyne salient, towards [Novokalynove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novokalynove) and [Keramik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keramik,_Ukraine), which were both likely captured by the end of April.[^cite_note-317] After capturing Ocheretyne and Keramik, the Russians further advanced northwards and captured [Arkhanhelske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arkhanhelske,_Donetsk_Oblast) by 5 May, according to DeepState; Russia later claimed the capture of Arkhanhelske on 25 May.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319]



Damaged buildings in [Chasiv Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chasiv_Yar), 30 March 2024

On 25 April, the ISW assessed that although Russia was seeking further "tactical gains" northwest of Avdiivka, [Chasiv Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chasiv_Yar) remained the primary target of the spring campaign.[^cite_note-320]

### Russian general offensive (April 2024–present)

#### Donetsk Oblast

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(January 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

See also: [Battle of Chasiv Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Chasiv_Yar), [Battle of Krasnohorivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Krasnohorivka), [Battle of Toretsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Toretsk), [Pokrovsk offensive](77627696.md), [Battle of Kurakhove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kurakhove), and [Velyka Novosilka offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velyka_Novosilka_offensive)

Russian media reported on 5 April that their troops had [entered the eastern suburbs of Chasiv Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Chasiv_Yar). [Ukraine's eastern command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operational_Command_East) denied these reports but admitted the situation on the city's outskirts had become "tense".[^cite_note-321] On 6 April, Colonel-General Oleksandr Syrskyi–who succeeded [Valerii Zaluzhnyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valerii_Zaluzhnyi) as [Ukraine's commander-in-chief](63525946.md) on 8 February–stated "Today, the fiercest battles are underway in the areas of Pervomaiske and Vodiane, as well as east of Chasiv Yar, where the enemy is trying to break through our defense lines."[^cite_note-322]

On 13 April 2024, Syrskyi stated the situation on the eastern front had "deteriorated significantly" in recent days and that fighting had intensified following the [2024 Russian presidential election](56748201.md). Syrskyi partially attributed recent Russian gains in Donetsk Oblast to their superior weaponry and spoke of the need for training of more Ukrainian infantry units.[^cite_note-323] The same day, [DeepStateMap.Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeepStateMap.Live) reported that Russian forces captured [Bohdanivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohdanivka,_Bakhmut_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), which is located less than 6 miles (10 km) east of Chasiv Yar; the Russian defence ministry later confirmed its capture on 21 April.[^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327]

Concurrent with the Chasiv Yar and Krasnohorivka battles, Russian troops also continued their ground advances on other axes in Donetsk into May 2024. Russian troops reportedly entered [Umanske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umanske) on 9 May 2024.[^cite_note-328] After a local offensive, Russian troops recaptured [Klishchiivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klishchiivka) and [Andriivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andriivka,_Bakhmut_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast) by 23 May, ending months of positional warfare after Ukraine retook the ruined villages in September 2023.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] Russia claimed to have captured the village of [Netailove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netailove) on 27 May 2024,located less than 15 kilometres northwest of Donetsk city's outskirts. Ukraine's General Staff reported "tense" clashes near Netailove, but did not confirm or deny the village's capture.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-27May24-332]

Russian soldiers operating [FPV drones](53703349.md#Evolution_into_additional_roles) against Ukrainian positions in [Donetsk Oblast](494889.md), 6 June 2024. Video released by the [Russian Ministry of Defence](7875884.md).

After months of clashes following the [fall of Marinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Marinka_\(2022%E2%80%932023\)), Russian sources reported the capture of [Heorhiivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heorhiivka,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast) around 14 June 2024.[^cite_note-333] The UK defence ministry stated in its 18 June 2024 intelligence update that Russian forces had likely captured [Novooleksandrivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novooleksandrivka,_Hrodivka_settlement_hromada) located just over 12 miles west of Avdiivka on the approach to the T-05-04 road. This has been one of the main supply routes for Ukrainian forces on the Donetsk front that links [Pokrovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pokrovsk,_Ukraine), [Kostiantynivka](2879020.md) and [Bakhmut](3889507.md).[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335]

On 18 June 2024, Russia launched a [new offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Toretsk) towards the city of [Toretsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toretsk),[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-18-336] and by 21 June had captured a village east of it, [Shumy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shumy).[^cite_note-ISW_2024-06-22-337][^cite_note-338] Fighting began in the [satellite settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toretsk_urban_hromada) east of Toretsk of [Pivdenne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pivdenne,_Donetsk_Oblast), [Zalizne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zalizne), [Druzhba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druzhba,_Donetsk_Oblast), and [Pivnichne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pivnichne,_Donetsk_Oblast), and by the end of June Russian forces had a foothold in all of these settlements.[^cite_note-ISW_2024-06-29-339][^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-13-340][^cite_note-ISW_2024-06-24-341] On 2 July, Russian forces advanced 4 kilometres (2.5 mi) north to reach and advance into southern [Niu-York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niu-York), a rural settlement south of Toretsk,[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-03-343] attempting to capture Toretsk from the east and south.[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-12-344]

Concurrent with the advances near Toretsk, Russian forces continued to expand the Avdiivka salient after the capture of Novooleksandrivka; [Sokil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sokil,_Donetsk_Oblast) by 6 July 2024[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-06-345] and [Voskhod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voskhod,_Donetsk_Oblast?action=edit\&redlink=1) by 11 July[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-11-346] were both captured, west of Ocheretyne. [Urozhaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urozhaine,_Donetsk_Oblast), east of [Staromaiorske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Staromaiorske,_Donetsk_Oblast), was taken around 13 July 2024,[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-13-340][^cite_note-DSML71324-347] and in the direction of [Siversk](6874321.md), Russian forces restarted offensive manoeuvres to capture [Spirne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spirne) and [Ivano-Daryivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivano-Daryivka) around 17 July.[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-19-348]

On 18 July 2024, Russian forces entered [Prohres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prohres), west of Ocheretyne, and captured it on 19 July,[^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-19-348] marking a new phase for the battle northwest of Avdiivka, as Russian forces [advanced towards](77627696.md) and hoped to seize the city of [Pokrovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pokrovsk,_Ukraine), a key Ukrainian logistical hub connecting two supply highways. Russian forces also seized the villages of [Lozuvatske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lozuvatske?action=edit\&redlink=1) and [Vovche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vovche,_Hrodivka_settlement_hromada,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), north and south of Prohres, in the week after the latter's capture. The situation, described as "tense and difficult", and Russian advances in the Pokrovsk front, were attributed to a lack of Ukrainian supplies and manpower.[^cite_note-356] On 24 July, a large mechanised assault was launched by Russia towards [Kurakhove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurakhove), with heavy losses;[^cite_note-357] another was launched by Russia towards [Kostiantynivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kostiantynivka,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast) in [Pokrovsk Raion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pokrovsk_Raion) the same day, again taking significant losses, but nevertheless gaining a foothold in the village.[^cite_note-WAPO72724-355][^cite_note-ISW_2024-07-25-358]

In August 2024, Russia continued making significant advances in the direction of Pokrovsk, while expanding their control in the area in and around Toretsk.[^cite_note-359] The Pokrovsk front had become the most active area of the frontline as Russia's main goal in eastern Ukraine by early August.[^cite_note-RBC-82-360][^cite_note-361] Following Ukraine's [incursion into Russia's Kursk Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2024_Kursk_Oblast_incursion) in early August 2024, the rate of Russian advance near Pokrovsk increased, where Russian forces at the axis of advance had been bolstered.[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-ECO-829-363] By mid-August, Russia was reportedly 10 kilometres from Pokrovsk,[^cite_note-364] and continued to advance through settlements east of the city, including in [Hrodivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hrodivka),[^cite_note-ISW_2024-08-15-365] a town situated on the last line of defence (a line running through Hrodivka–Novohrodivka–Selydove north to south) ahead of Pokrovsk itself.[^cite_note-366] In late August, Russia entered Toretsk from two axes of advance, entering it from Pivnichne to the east and Zalizne to the south.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] On 27 August, Russia captured the key city of [Novohrodivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novohrodivka), nine kilometres east of Pokrovsk,[^cite_note-ECO-829-363][^cite_note-369] in an engagement reportedly lasting only three days.[^cite_note-KP828-370] The following day, fighting reportedly began over [Selydove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selydove) as Russian forces entered the city from the east, capturing parts of the [E50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_route_E50) highway southeast of Pokrovsk in the process.[^cite_note-ISW_2024-08-28-371][^cite_note-372]

On 27 August 2024 Russia concurrently captured significant portions of the highway running from [Marinka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marinka,_Donetsk_Oblast) to [Vuhledar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vuhledar), and likely seized in entirety the village of Kostiantynivka intersected by the road.[^cite_note-ISW_2024-08-28-371][^cite_note-ISW_August_21-373] In late August, a [renewed offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Vuhledar#Renewed_offensive_efforts_and_capture_\(August%E2%80%93October_2024\)) was launched towards Vuhledar.[^cite_note-374] The villages of [Prechystivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prechystivka) and [Vodiane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodiane,_Volnovakha_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast) were captured on 3 and 8 September 2024, west and northeast of Vuhledar,[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] while assaults near Vuhledar itself arose.[^cite_note-378] Russian forces encircled the city, hampering the transport of Ukrainian supplies into the city and the ability of defending soldiers to move out of it.[^cite_note-379] The city was captured by Russia on 1 October.[^cite_note-telegraph101-380][^cite_note-occupied1-381]



Apartment building in [Bilozerske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilozerske) (Donetsk Oblast) after Russian drone strike on 30 May 2025

On 9 September 2024, the city of [Krasnohorivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnohorivka) was [captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Krasnohorivka).[^cite_note-ISW_2024-09-10-382] On 17 September, Russia [captured](77627696.md#Ukrainsk) the city of [Ukrainsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainsk), southeast of Pokrovsk,[^cite_note-ISW_2024-09-17-383][^cite_note-NYT918-384] after their forces had reportedly entered the city by 6 September 2024.[^cite_note-385] In September, nearby Ukrainsk, fighting began over the settlement [Tsukuryne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsukuryne)[^cite_note-deepstate-386] and the city of [Hirnyk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hirnyk,_Donetsk_Oblast),[^cite_note-evac-387] as Russian forces pressed further towards [Kurakhove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurakhove).[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-british-389] By 10 October, Russia captured the settlement of [Tsukuryne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsukuryne) south of Selydove,[^cite_note-390] and [Mykolaivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykolaivka,_Hrodivka_settlement_hromada,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), [Krasnyi Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krasnyi_Yar,_Hrodivka_settlement_hromada,_Pokrovsk_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast), [Krutyi Yar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krutyi_Yar?action=edit\&redlink=1) next to [Myrnohrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myrnohrad).[^cite_note-tsukuryne-391] Russia captured the cities of [Selydove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selydove)[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393] and Hirnyk in late October.[^cite_note-394]

East of Kurakhove, Russian forces took full control over [Maksymilianivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maksymilianivka) in mid-October 2024,[^cite_note-395] as well as the settlement of [Ostrivske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ostrivske) at the Kurakhove reservoir. After the village's capture, [fighting over Kurakhove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Kurakhove) began.[^cite_note-396] Russian forces seized the mining settlement of [Kurakhivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurakhivka)[^cite_note-397] and advanced into Kurakhove proper in late October.[^cite_note-398]

After the capture of Vuhledar, Russian forces exploited the city's fall to make significant advances north and northwest of it;[^cite_note-399] the settlements of [Bohoiavlenka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohoiavlenka),[^cite_note-400] [Novoukrainka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novoukrainka,_Vuhledar_urban_hromada,_Volnovakha_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast),[^cite_note-401] [Yasna Poliana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yasna_Poliana,_Volnovakha_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-402] [Shakhtarske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shakhtarske,_Donetsk_Oblast?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-403] and [Maksymivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maksymivka,_Donetsk_Oblast?action=edit\&redlink=1)[^cite_note-404] were captured in late October to early November 2024.

In late December 2024, Russian forces captured Kurakhove after two months of fighting.[^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406] The [Kurakhove Power Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurakhove_Power_Station) was confirmed to have come under Russian control in early January 2025.[^cite_note-407] In late January, Russian forces [captured](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Velyka_Novosilka_offensive) the fortified settlement Velyka Novosilka.[^cite_note-control-408]

#### Northern Kharkiv Oblast

|  | This section needs to be **updated**. Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information. *(May 2024)* |
| --- | --- |

Main article: [2024 Kharkiv offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kharkiv_offensive)

Damaged and destroyed buildings in [Vovchansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vovchansk), 2 June 2024

On 10 May 2024, as Russian forces continued their gradual advances in Donetsk and Luhansk oblasts, they launched a new offensive in Ukraine's [Kharkiv Oblast](494901.md), using shelling, air strikes and dismounted infantry to breach defences in the [Vovchansk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vovchansk) and [Kharkiv](2788558.md) directions.[^cite_note-409] On 17 May, Col. Gen. Oleksandr Syrskyi stated that the new offensive "expanded the zone of active hostilities by almost 70 kilometers."[^cite_note-PutinKharkiv-410] By 21 May, Russia had reoccupied 13[^cite_note-411] villages and settlements in Kharkiv Oblast and had advanced up to the northern bank of the [Vovcha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vovcha_\(Donets\)) river in Vovchansk.[^cite_note-ISW13-412][^cite_note-ISW20-413]

Early speculation Regarding the purpose of the operation ranged from it being an intent to capture Kharkiv to it serving as a feint to stretch Ukrainian defence lines, forcing them to shift reserves away from other fronts, particularly the Donbas front.[^cite_note-414] On 17 May, President Vladimir Putin stated that Russia was aiming to create a "buffer zone" in Kharkiv Oblast to prevent further attacks on [Belgorod](478857.md) while denying that there were plans to capture Kharkiv city. Earlier that same week, Ukrainian president Volodymyr Zelensky stated the Russian advance was stopped at the first line of defence,[^cite_note-24May2024-415] while Ukrainian intelligence chief [Kyrylo Budanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyrylo_Budanov) reportedly warned that Russia may launch a new ground incursion into [Sumy Oblast](1567618.md).[^cite_note-PutinKharkiv-410]

By 23 May, Russian troops had become "completely bogged down" amid urban combat in Vovchansk, according to Syrskyi.[^cite_note-24May2024-415] On 24 May, Ukraine's General Staff stated that Ukrainian forces had "halted" the Kharkiv offensive and were conducting counterattacks, adding that the most intense fighting in Ukraine was occurring in Kharkiv and Donetsk oblasts.[^cite_note-24May2024-415]

#### Luhansk Oblast and eastern Kharkiv Oblast

Main article: [Luhansk Oblast campaign § Renewed offensive towards Kupiansk (November 2023–present)](71944889.md#Renewed_offensive_towards_Kupiansk_\(November_2023%E2%80%93present\))

On 20 May, the Russian defence ministry stated it had fully recaptured [Bilohorivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilohorivka,_Luhansk_Oblast) in [Luhansk Oblast](494904.md), however Ukraine's General Staff stated Ukrainian forces were still "holding back" Russian forces near the settlement.[^cite_note-416]

On 27 May 2024, Russia announced the capture of the village of Ivanivka in northeastern Kharkiv Oblast, located around 20 kilometres east of [Kupiansk](5156384.md).[^cite_note-27May24-332] On 9 June, the [Institute for the Study of War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_for_the_Study_of_War) assessed that Russian forces had "likely recently seized" the village of [Ivanivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivanivka_\(Petropavlovsk_rural_community\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) in the Kharkiv Oblast citing geolocated footage.[^cite_note-417]

On 20 July 2024, Russia was confirmed to have recaptured [Pishchane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pishchane,_Kharkiv_Oblast) in eastern Kharkiv Oblast.[^cite_note-418]

On 29 August 2024, footage showed that Russia had made advances in central Synkivka, with some sources saying the village had been captured.[^cite_note-419] On 30 August, Russia seized the village of [Stelmakhivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stelmakhivka).[^cite_note-420]

By 6 September 2024, Russian forces had completely seized Synkivka.[^cite_note-421] Further advances south in the ensuing week were made into northern [Petropavlivka](72311698.md).[^cite_note-422]

By 10 October 2024, Russian forces captured [Myasozharivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myasozharivka).[^cite_note-tsukuryne-391]

## Casualties

You can help by [adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Eastern_front_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=)[adding missing information](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Eastern_front_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_\(2022%E2%80%93present\)\&action=edit\&section=) or [making an edit request](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Talk%3AEastern_front_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_war_%282022%E2%80%93present%29\&preload=Template%3ASubmit+an+edit+request%2Fpreload\&action=edit\&section=new\&editintro=Template%3AEdit+extended-protected%2Feditintro\&preloadtitle=Extended-confirmed-protected+edit+request+on+3+October+2026\&preloadparams%5B%5D=edit+extended-protected\&preloadparams%5B%5D=Eastern+front+of+the+Russo-Ukrainian+war+%282022%E2%80%93present%29). *(September 2023)* |

See also: [Casualties of the Russo-Ukrainian War § Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War#Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

### Military casualties

According to Ukrainian sources, more than 500 wounded Russian soldiers, most of them recently mobilised, were hospitalised in the occupied city of [Horlivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horlivka) between 10 and 16 November 2022.[^cite_note-484]

On 1 October 2023, a Ukrainian sniper team of 20 men called the "Ghosts of Bakhmut", claimed that they had killed 558 Russians during the battle so far, with their leader (callsign "Ghost") reportedly being responsible for 113 of them. They also claimed that only two of them had been wounded, with Ghost again being one of them.[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486]\
On 19 August 2025, "Ghost" claimed that his snipers had "destroyed" more than a thousand Russian soldiers over the past year, and that one of them had "eliminated" two Russian servicemen at a distance of 4,000 metres.[^cite_note-i574-487]

As of January 2024, Ukrainian obituaries recorded the names of 7,644 Ukrainian servicemen killed during the battle of Bakhmut.[^cite_note-488]

In April 2024, the Ukrainian [Khortytsia Joint Task Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khortytsia_operational-strategic_group) claimed that Russia had suffered "total losses", only in the eastern direction, of 24,156 personnel, 308 tanks, 671 armoured vehicles, 575 guns and mortars, 19 MLRs, 871 vehicles, plus various other equipment systems.[^cite_note-489]

In May 2024, the Ukrainians claimed that Russian losses in the eastern direction were 25,665 personnel, 408 tanks, 814 AFVs, 816 guns and mortars, 26 MLRs, 1,363 vehicles, 79 anti-tank weapons, 5 heavy flamethrowers, 20 AA systems, 105 pieces of "special equipment", plus numerous other equipment types.[^cite_note-490]

In June 2024, the Khortytsia group again reported on Russian losses in the east, claiming that they had "eliminated" 31,936 personnel, 342 enemy tanks, 592 armoured fighting vehicles, 1,084 guns and mortars, 13 MLRs, 1,302 other vehicles, 80 anti-tank weapons, 44 air defence systems, a heavy flamethrower system and one aircraft, plus various other weapons systems.[^cite_note-491]

In July 2024, the Ukrainians claimed that Russian total losses were 31,748 soldiers (including 6,460 killed in a week between 15 and 22 July) in the eastern sector alone. Claimed equipment losses were 282 tanks, 608 AFVs, 1,121 guns and mortars, 14 MLRs, 5 heavy flamethrowers, 64 anti-tank weapons, 5 AA guns, 1,423 vehicles, 264 pieces of "special equipment" and 3 planes plus other equipment.[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493]

In August 2024, Ukraine claimed to have inflicted 32,988 casualties on the Russians that month, with 184 tanks, 502 AFVs, 1,143 artillery systems, 34 MLRs, 51 anti-tank weapons, 1,510 vehicles, 397 special equipments, 10 AA batteries, 2 heavy flamethrowers plus other equipment.[^cite_note-494]

The ISW reported that Russian forces had suffered an estimated 80,110 casualties during intensified offensive efforts against Kupyansk in Kharkiv Oblast and Selydove, Kurakhove, and Vuhledar in Donetsk Oblast in September and October 2024, or "roughly 20,000 more casualties than US forces suffered during almost 20 years of operations in [Iraq](5043324.md) and [Afghanistan](19666611.md)". According to UK Defense Secretary John Healey, Russia lost 1,345 troops per day, or about 41,980 casualties, in October 2024, with 1,271 daily losses or about 38,130 casualties in September. Data compiled by Oryx indicates that Russian forces also lost 197 tanks, 661 armoured personnel carriers (APCs), and 65 artillery systems larger than 100mm, while they had seized and recaptured 1,517 square kilometres of territory, "an area less than a third the size of Delaware".[^cite_note-q901-495]

The Khortytsia group claimed that the Russians had suffered "colossal" losses of over 35,000 casualties in their operational zone during November 2024. The ISW reported that Ukrainian military observer Petro Chernyk claimed on 3 December that Russian forces are losing 1,700 troops per day and suffered 45,000 total casualties in November 2024 "as they attempted to maintain intensified offensive operations in eastern Ukraine". The UK MoD agreed with this assessment, claiming that Russian forces suffered an estimated 45,690 total casualties, suffering 1,523 casualties per day in November 2024 and just over 2,000 casualties in a single day for the first time on 28 November 2024. In addition, 307 Russian tanks, 899 combat vehicles, and 884 artillery systems were claimed to be destroyed.[^cite_note-k310-496] The ISW further reported that Russian forces had suffered an estimated 125,800 casualties "during a period of intensified offensive operations" in September, October, and November 2024 in exchange for 2,356 square kilometres of gains, "approximately 53 Russian casualties per square kilometer of Ukrainian territory seized".[^cite_note-q961-497][^cite_note-a626-498][^cite_note-o063-499]

On 3 December, LtCol Nazar Voloshyn claimed that Russian forces had lost 9,000 casualties and 40 tanks in eastern Ukraine over the previous week.[^cite_note-a626-498]

### Civilian casualties

According to [Lyudmyla Denisova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyudmyla_Denisova), the Commissioner for Human Rights in Ukraine, four civilians were killed when a shell hit their home during the night of 9 March in the village of [Slobozhanske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slobozhanske,_Chuhuiv_Raion,_Kharkiv_Oblast), located in [Izium Raion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Izium_Raion).[^cite_note-9march-500]

Russian shelling on [Derhachi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derhachi) on 11 March reportedly killed three civilians.[^cite_note-11March2-501]

On 14 March, two civilians were reportedly killed in Russian shelling on houses in [Kharkiv](2788558.md), and a child was killed after Russian shelling hit a kindergarten in [Chuhuiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chuhuiv).[^cite_note-14March-502] On 17 March, at least 21 people were reportedly killed following Russian shelling in [Merefa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merefa).[^cite_note-21killed-503]
